L1311: *-S9 without metabolic activation, +S9 with metabolic activation, * Based on 10 mg apixaban per day.
L1312: ## Conclusions:
L1313: - Although most of the eight impurities, except [REDACTED](b) (4) and [REDACTED](b) (4) were present at levels less than [REDACTED](b) (4)% or the specified level in lots used for general toxicology studies, the exposure level of each impurity in animals was at least 90-fold higher than the maximum predicted exposure level in humans (Appendix 2). Therefore, the eight apixaban impurities are considered qualified in terms of general toxicology.
- The FDA Computational Toxicology analysis predicted impurity [REDACTED](b) (4) to be positive for the Ames assay. Although the sponsor synthesized impurity [REDACTED](b) (4) and could potentially evaluate this impurity directly in an Ames assay, such an evaluation is not considered necessary, because: (a) [REDACTED](b) (4) was present at adequate levels in the Ames assay of the apixaban drug substance and (b) the reliability of the positive prediction in the Ames assay by Computational Toxicology is weak.
L1314: 3. The FDA Computational Toxicology analysis predicted impurity [REDACTED](b) (4) to be positive for the micronucleus assay. Although [REDACTED](b) (4) was not present at adequate concentrations in the apixaban in vivo micronucleus assay, chromosomal aberrations were evaluated in a 1-month repeat dose study using an apixaban lot in which [REDACTED](b) (4) was present at [REDACTED](b) (4)%. The high dose contained a [REDACTED](b) (4) human equivalent dose of 58 µg/kg/day or 362-fold the maximum possible human exposure to [REDACTED](b) (4). Therefore, the clastogenic potential of [REDACTED](b) (4) was adequately tested and an additional in vivo study for clastogenicity is not necessary.
L1315: 4. The levels of six compounds in the apixaban synthesis identified as genotoxic are controlled [REDACTED](b) (4) in the manufacturing process of apixaban. Spiking/purging studies confirmed that each of the genotoxic compounds was adequately purged and the level of each genotoxic compound in the final apixaban drug substance is &lt;10 ppm. Not only is the maximum exposure of each compound, but also is the total exposure of all six compounds to a patient taking the maximum dose of 10 mg of apixaban per day less than the Threshold of Toxicological concern of 1.5 µg per day. Therefore, the levels of the identified genotoxic compounds are being adequately controlled.
L1316: ## Recommendations:
L1317: The reviewer recommends that the apixaban impurities identified by HPLC be considered qualified and no additional toxicology or genetic toxicology study be required. In addition, the identified genotoxic impurities are considered adequately controlled.

L1324: ## Appendix 3: FDA Computational Toxicology Evaluation
L1325: To: Patricia Harlow
cc: Thomas Papoian
From: CDER/OPS/OTR/DDSR: The CDER Computational Toxicology Group
Re: NDA 202155
Date: October 20, 2011
L1326: Twenty-two known and potential structures of metabolites and impurities of apixaban were evaluated by CDER/OPS/OTR/DDSR for genetic toxicity against the ICH S2 battery. Four (quantitative) structure-activity relationship [(Q)SAR] computational toxicology software programs were used: Derek for Windows 13 (DfW), Leadscope Model Applier 1.3.3-3 (LMA), MC4PC 2.4.0.7 (MC), and SciQSAR 2.2 (SQ).
L1327: The results of the predictions from the software programs were weighted equally and the analysis was optimized for sensitivity (minimizing false negatives) to reach the overall conclusions. The overall genetox predictions for the 22 structures are shown in the first summary table below, followed by predictions for each chemical in detail. All 22 chemical structures are shown in Figure 1.
L1328: The predicted toxicological activities are scored as follows in all tables:
L1329: +	Positive
Eqv	Equivocal/marginally active
-	Negative
NSA	No structural alerts identified by DfW
NC	No call can be made because the chemical's structural features are not adequately represented in the model (poor coverage)
N/A	No model available for this endpoint with this software

L1364: Figure 2 is a scatter plot showing C_{max} (ng/mL) on the Y-axis versus Dialysis (No/Yes) on the X-axis. Data points are categorized by administration route: PO (Oral, represented by triangles) and IV (Intravenous, represented by circles). The legend indicates: \Delta PO and \bullet IV. The plot illustrates that C_{max} values generally decrease when dialysis is performed, particularly for the PO route. A note below the plot states: *Note: Measurements obtained from the same subject are connected by a dashed (PO) or a dotted (IV) line.
L1365: Figure 2 illustrates two models of coagulation: A: Cascade model (Adams and Bird, 2009) and B: Contemporary model (Tanaka et al. 2009).
A: Cascade model (Adams and Bird, 2009)
This model shows the classical coagulation cascade initiated by a damaged surface (Intrinsic pathway, APTT) or Trauma (Extrinsic pathway, PT).
Intrinsic pathway (APTT): Damaged surface activates Kininogen/Kallikrein, which activates XII and XI. XII activates XI. XI activates IX and X. IX activates X. X is activated by Xa.
Extrinsic pathway (PT): Trauma activates Tissue factor (TF). TF activates VII. VII activates X.
Common pathway: X and Xa activate Prothrombin to Thrombin. Thrombin converts Fibrinogen to Fibrin.
B: Contemporary model (Tanaka et al. 2009)
This model focuses on coagulation initiation and propagation on activated platelets.
Initiation: fVIIa/TF activates fX.
Propagation: fX activates fXa. fXa forms a complex with fVa (fVa/fXa) on platelet surfaces, leading to fXIIa activation. fXIIa activates fXIa. fXIa activates fX (amplification loop). fX also activates fXa (amplification loop).
Amplification and Regulation: fXa and fXIIa are regulated by AT (Antithrombin) and TFPI (Tissue Factor Pathway Inhibitor). fXIIa also activates fibrin polymerization.
Hemophilia: A defect in the fVIIa/TF to fX step is indicated.
L1366: Line graph showing Platelet Aggregation (%) versus Time (min) for Vehicle and various concentrations of Apixaban (0.1, 1, 10, 100, 1000 nM). The Vehicle group shows high aggregation (around 85%) within 5 minutes. Apixaban concentrations of 0.1 nM and 1 nM show minimal inhibition. Higher concentrations (10, 100, 1000 nM) show increasing inhibition, with 100 nM and 1000 nM significantly reducing aggregation.
L1367: Figure 4 displays bar charts illustrating the effects of apixaban on plasma thrombotic biomarkers (T-AT and sCD40L) in db/db mice compared to control C57BL/6 mice (C57). The Y-axis for both charts represents Plasma T-AT (% of Vehicle) and Plasma sCD40L (% of Vehicle), ranging from 0 to 200. The X-axis shows treatment groups: C57 (control), Vehicle (db/db), and Apixaban (db/db).
The left panel shows results for Twice daily dosing of apixaban (Apixaban po bid (mg/kg)).
T-AT (Left Chart): C57 mice show low levels (~30%). Vehicle treated db/db mice show high levels (~100%). Apixaban 5 mg/kg (light gray bar) and 50 mg/kg (dark gray bar) both significantly reduce T-AT levels compared to Vehicle (indicated by *P<0.05 vs. Vehicle). Apixaban 50 mg/kg shows the lowest level (~40%).
sCD40L (Middle Chart): C57 mice show low levels (~30%). Vehicle treated db/db mice show high levels (~100%). Apixaban 5 mg/kg (light gray bar) and 50 mg/kg (dark gray bar) both significantly reduce sCD40L levels compared to Vehicle (indicated by *P<0.05 vs. Vehicle). Apixaban 50 mg/kg shows the lowest level (~30%).
The right panel shows results for Once daily dosing (Apixaban po qd (mg/kg)).
sCD40L (Right Chart): C57 mice show low levels (~30%). Vehicle treated db/db mice show high levels (~100%). Apixaban 10 mg/kg (light gray bar) and 100 mg/kg (dark gray bar) both significantly reduce sCD40L levels compared to Vehicle (indicated by *P<0.05 vs. Vehicle). Apixaban 10 mg/kg shows the lowest level (~140%).
Legend: White bar: control C57BL/6 mice, gray bar: db/db mice treated with vehicle, darker or black bars: db/db mice treated with apixaban.
L1368: Figure 5 displays three bar charts illustrating the effects of various drug combinations on integrated blood flow, thrombus weight, and bleeding time.
Integrated Blood Flow (% control): The chart shows that Apixaban (Api) ED50 + Aspirin (ASA) significantly increases blood flow compared to Vehicle (*). Apixaban ED20 + ASA also shows a significant increase (*#). Clopidogrel (Clopi) alone shows a moderate increase (*). Apixaban ED20 + ASA + Clopi shows a significant increase (*#). Apixaban ED50 + ASA + Clopi shows a significant increase (*#). Apixaban ED80 + ASA + Clopi shows a significant increase (*#).
Thrombus Weight (mg): The chart shows that Apixaban ED20 + ASA significantly reduces thrombus weight compared to Vehicle (*). Apixaban ED50 + ASA significantly reduces thrombus weight compared to Apixaban ED20 + ASA (*#). Apixaban ED80 + ASA significantly reduces thrombus weight compared to Apixaban ED50 + ASA (*#). Apixaban ED20 + ASA + Clopi significantly reduces thrombus weight compared to Apixaban ED80 + ASA (*#). Apixaban ED50 + ASA + Clopi significantly reduces thrombus weight compared to Apixaban ED20 + ASA + Clopi (*#). Apixaban ED80 + ASA + Clopi significantly reduces thrombus weight compared to Apixaban ED50 + ASA + Clopi (*#).
Bleeding Time (s): The chart shows that Clopidogrel (Clopi) alone significantly increases bleeding time compared to Vehicle (*). Apixaban ED20 + ASA + Clopi significantly increases bleeding time compared to Apixaban ED80 + ASA (*#). Apixaban ED50 + ASA + Clopi significantly increases bleeding time compared to Apixaban ED20 + ASA + Clopi (*#). Apixaban ED80 + ASA + Clopi significantly increases bleeding time compared to Apixaban ED50 + ASA + Clopi (*#).
Aspirin (ASA) was given at 1 mg/kg/h i.v. Apixaban (Api) was studied at 0.04 (ED20), 0.3 (ED50) and 2.1 (ED80) mg/kg/h i.v. Clopidogrel (Clopi) was dosed orally at 3 mg/kg/d. *P < 0.05, compared to aspirin; #P < 0.05, compared with the preceding group and +P < 0.05, compared to ASA plus Clopi. Means \pm SE and n = 6 per group.
L1369: Figure 6 illustrates the metabolic pathways of Apixaban (M1, BMS-730823) in humans and other species. The central molecule is Apixaban, which undergoes various metabolic transformations.
Principal Pathway (Highlighted in Red):
Apixaban is metabolized to O-Desmethyl Apixaban (M2, BMS-566665).
O-Desmethyl Apixaban (M2) is further metabolized to O-Desmethyl Apixaban Sulfate (M1, BMS-730823).
Other Metabolic Pathways:
Apixaban is metabolized to Apixaban Carboxylic Acid (M6).
Apixaban is metabolized to 3-Hydroxy Apixaban (M7).
Apixaban is metabolized to Hydroxy Apixaban (M4).
Apixaban is metabolized to Apixaban Oxide-1 & -2 (M9, M16).
Apixaban is metabolized to Hydroxy O-Desmethyl Apixaban (M8).
Apixaban is metabolized to Hydroxy O-Desmethyl Apixaban (M13).
Apixaban is metabolized to O-Desmethyl Apixaban Glucuronide-1 (M14).
Apixaban is metabolized to O-Desmethyl Apixaban Glucuronide-2 (M15).
Apixaban is metabolized to Hydroxy O-Desmethyl Apixaban Sulfate-1 & -2 (M10, M11).
Notes:
* indicates ^{14}\text{C} location.
Gluc: glucuronic acid.
M3, M5: result of opening of the keto-lactam ring via oxidation and hydrolysis, respectively.
L1370: Line graph showing the recovery of radioactivity (% of dose) over time (h) for Urine, Feces, and Total excretion in mice. Urine excretion increases rapidly, reaching about 18% at 12h and 20% at 48h. Feces excretion increases rapidly, reaching about 75% at 12h and 80% at 48h. Total excretion reaches 100% at 48h.
L1371: Figure 8 displays two graphs showing the recovery of radioactivity (% of dose) over time (h) in rabbits, comparing intravenous (IV) and oral dosing.
Intravenous dosing (Left Graph): The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 90%. The X-axis ranges from 0 to 48 h. Urine recovery increases steadily, reaching approximately 85% at 48 h. Feces recovery increases, reaching approximately 60% at 48 h. Cage Debris and Total recovery remain low (around 20-25% and 10-15%, respectively).
Oral dosing (Right Graph): The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 50%. The X-axis ranges from 0 to 48 h. Urine recovery increases steadily, reaching approximately 50% at 48 h. Feces recovery increases, reaching approximately 50% at 48 h. Cage Debris and Total recovery remain low (around 5% and 5%, respectively).
Both graphs show that Urine and Feces are the primary routes of excretion for radioactivity in rabbits following both IV and oral administration.

L1373: The figure displays two Kaplan-Meier survival curves: one for Males and one for Females, comparing five groups (1 to 5) over time (Day).
The legend indicates:
1: Control 1
2: Control 2
3: 150 mg/kg/d
4: 500 mg/kg/d
5: males 1500 mg/kg/d, females 3000 mg/kg/d
The curves show that survival decreases over time for all groups, with Group 5 (highest dose) generally showing the lowest survival rate, particularly in males.
L1374: Figure 11 displays two line graphs showing Mean Body Weight (g) versus Study Weeks for males and females in Study DN05068. The left graph (Male) shows body weight increasing steadily from Study Weeks -1 to 16, with all groups (Control 1, Control 2, 150 mg/kg, 500 mg/kg, 1500 mg/kg) showing similar trends, with the 500 mg/kg group slightly lower. The right graph (Female) shows body weight increasing up to Study Week 55, followed by a slight decrease. The 3000 mg/kg group shows a significant decrease in body weight compared to control groups starting around Study Week 14, reaching approximately 40 g by Study Week 60, while control groups remain around 45 g.

L1376: Figure 12 displays four line graphs illustrating Food Consumption (g/animal/day) over Study Weeks (Weeks 1-16 and Weeks 20-104) for Male and Female animals across four dose groups: 0 mg/kg/day (Control), 150 mg/kg/day, 500 mg/kg/day, and 1500/3000 mg/kg/day.

0 mg/kg/day (Control)
150 mg/kg/day
500 mg/kg/day
1500/3000 mg/kg/day
The graphs show that consumption generally fluctuates around 5-6 g/animal/day, with a notable peak around Week 8 (approximately 8.5 g/animal/day) for all groups in the initial 16 weeks. Consumption tends to stabilize or slightly decrease in the later weeks (Weeks 20-104).

L1378: Figure 14 displays two graphs showing Apixaban plasma concentrations (ng/mL) over time (Hours post start of dark cycle) for male and female mice across three dose levels (150 mg/kg, 500 mg/kg, and 1500 mg/kg for males; 150 mg/kg, 500 mg/kg, and 3000 mg/kg for females). The graphs illustrate the pharmacokinetics of Apixaban in mice, showing concentration peaks and troughs over a 24-hour cycle.
L1379: The figure displays two Kaplan-Meier survival curves for males and females in Study DN05069, showing Percent Survival versus Day (0 to 800).
The legend indicates five groups:
1: Control
2: Control
3: 50 mg/kg/d
4: 200 mg/kg/d
5: 600 mg/kg/d
The curves show that survival rates are generally high (around 90-100%) until approximately Day 400, after which they decline. The decline appears slightly accelerated in the treated groups (3, 4, 5) compared to the control groups (1, 2), although the differences are not dramatic.
L1380: Figure 16 displays four graphs showing mean body weight (Weight, g) versus Study Weeks for male and female rats across five dose groups (0 mg/kg/day, 50 mg/kg/day, 200 mg/kg/day, and 600 mg/kg/day) in Study DN05069.
The graphs are arranged in a 2x2 grid:
Top Left: Males, Weeks 1 to 16.
Top Right: Males, Weeks 20 to 104.
Bottom Left: Females, Weeks 1 to 16.
Bottom Right: Females, Weeks 20 to 104.
The legend indicates the following dose groups:
0 mg/kg/day (Control 1)
0 mg/kg/day (Control 2)
50 mg/kg/day
200 mg/kg/day
600 mg/kg/day
The graphs illustrate that body weight generally increases over the study period, with minimal differences observed between the treatment groups during the initial phase (Weeks 1-16). During the later phase (Weeks 20-104), the 600 mg/kg/day group shows a noticeable decrease in mean body weight compared to the control groups, particularly in males.
L1381: Figure 17 displays four line graphs showing Food Consumption (g/animal/day) versus Study Weeks for Male and Female animals across five dose groups: 0 mg/kg/day (Control 1), 0 mg/kg/day (Control 2), 0 mg/kg/day (Control 1 & 2), 50 mg/kg/day, 200 mg/kg/day, and 600 mg/kg/day.
The top row shows data for Male animals, and the bottom row shows data for Female animals. The X-axis represents Study Weeks (1 to 16 for the top row, 20 to 104 for the bottom row). The Y-axis represents Consumption (g/animal/day).
The graphs illustrate the fluctuating nature of food consumption over the study period. For both Male and Female animals, the 50 mg/kg/day, 200 mg/kg/day, and 600 mg/kg/day groups generally show consumption levels slightly above the 0 mg/kg/day control groups, although significant fluctuations occur, particularly in the later weeks (Weeks 32 and 64).
L1382: Figure 18 displays two line graphs showing Mean compound consumption (mg/kg/day) versus Week (0 to 100) for Males (left) and Females (right). The graphs compare three groups: Group 5 (highest dose, blue diamonds), Group 4 (middle dose, green squares), and Group 3 (lowest dose, red circles).
Males: Group 5 consumption ranges from approximately 500 to 700 mg/kg/day. Group 4 ranges from approximately 180 to 240 mg/kg/day. Group 3 ranges from approximately 45 to 60 mg/kg/day.
Females: Group 5 consumption ranges from approximately 300 to 700 mg/kg/day. Group 4 ranges from approximately 150 to 220 mg/kg/day. Group 3 ranges from approximately 40 to 60 mg/kg/day.
